# Accuracy

Relative error versus mpmath of xsf-rust 0.7.0-dev, the C++ [xsf](https://github.com/scipy/xsf)
it is translated from (commit [`0f44f5d`](https://github.com/scipy/xsf/tree/0f44f5dd995a67d806b9ef0cbbac469f2b2425a0)), and
the Rust standard library where it has the function. Where xsf lacks a function, SciPy 1.18.1
or NumPy 2.5.4 takes its place.

- **peak**: the largest error; **gmean**: the geometric mean of the finite errors, each at least
  $`2^{-53}`$ (0 if all are 0)
- **∞ (k)**: $`k`$ infinite errors, e.g. NaN, or a spurious overflow
- **bold**: the smallest peak or gmean of a row (unless all are equal at the displayed precision)
- **❗**: an error of $`10^{-6}`$ or more, or infinite errors
- **—**: in neither xsf nor SciPy (with the same definition); footnotes mark the rows with
  SciPy or NumPy instead of xsf
- **same**: the share of inputs where xsf-rust and xsf (or SciPy) return the same f64
  (bit-identical)
- **(log)**: sampled log-uniformly; **$`\pm`$**: with a random sign

For reference, $`\varepsilon`$ = 2.2e-16. See [Method](#method) for the details.

<sup>†</sup> Ill-conditioned near the zeros.

<sup>‡</sup> Element $`k`$ of the outputs for `N = 500` (counting from 1): the zero $`k`$, or the value there.

<sup>*</sup> For $`x > \lvert v\rvert`$: $`A = \sqrt{J_v(x)^2 + Y_v(x)^2}`$.

<sup>†</sup> $`A = \sqrt{\lvert J_v(z)\rvert^2 + \lvert Y_v(z)\rvert^2}`$.

<sup>‡</sup> For $`\lvert z\rvert > \lvert v\rvert`$: $`A = s\sqrt{\lvert J_v(z)\rvert^2 + \lvert Y_v(z)\rvert^2}`$, with $`s = e^{-\lvert\mathrm{Im}\,z\rvert}`$ for `bessel_je` and `bessel_ye`, else 1.

<sup>§</sup> Ill-conditioned near the zeros.

<sup>‖</sup> Reference: the mpmath root of the forward reference. Ill-conditioned where the forward function is flat, e.g. in its tails.

<sup>¶</sup> The last order, $`v`$. For $`x > u`$, with $`u = v`$, or $`v + 1`$ for the derivative: $`A = \Gamma(v + 1) (2/x)^v \sqrt{J_u(x)^2 + Y_u(x)^2}`$.

The zeros of $`J_1`$ and $`J_0' = -J_1`$ come in either order, by rounding; they are compared as if $`J_1`$ is first.

<sup>*</sup> Ill-conditioned near the zeros, where the largest errors are.

<sup>†</sup> Ill-conditioned near the zeros.

<sup>‡</sup> Ill-conditioned for negative non-integer $`a`$ with large $`\lvert a\rvert`$, and near the poles of $`\Gamma(a + b)`$ (or $`\Gamma(a + m)`$), where the largest errors are.

<sup>§</sup> $`\mu = a / (a + b)`$, $`\sigma^2 = \mu(1 - \mu) / (a + b + 1)`$.

<sup>‖</sup> Reference: the mpmath root of the forward reference. Ill-conditioned where the forward function is flat, e.g. in its tails.

<sup>¶</sup> The second column is SciPy.

<sup>**</sup> The second column is SciPy, which uses Boost.Math here.

<sup>*</sup> For $`z < 0`$ with $`x = 2\sqrt{-z} > \lvert b - 1\rvert`$: $`A = \lvert\Gamma(b)\rvert (x/2)^{1-b} \sqrt{J_{b-1}(x)^2 + Y_{b-1}(x)^2}`$.

<sup>†</sup> For $`4\lvert z\rvert > (b - 1)^2`$, with $`x = 2\sqrt{-z}`$: $`A = \lvert\Gamma(b)\rvert \lvert x/2\rvert^{1-b} \sqrt{\lvert J_{b-1}(x)\rvert^2 + \lvert Y_{b-1}(x)\rvert^2}`$.

<sup>‡</sup> Ill-conditioned near the zeros.

<sup>§</sup> inf (k) counts the NaN results, where its error estimate says that none of its digits are correct.

<sup>‖</sup> inf (k) counts the `None` results, where it cannot compute the result to a relative error of about $`10^{-13}`$.

<sup>*</sup> Reference of this section: the eigenvalues and eigenvectors of the recurrence relations [DLMF 28.4.5-8](https://dlmf.nist.gov/28.4#ii), the Fourier series [28.4.1-4](https://dlmf.nist.gov/28.4#i), and the series of Bessel function products [28.24.2-5](https://dlmf.nist.gov/28.24), in mpmath.

<sup>†</sup> For some $`m`$ and $`q`$, the result is the characteristic value of another order of the same parity, which the other functions of this section then use. For $`m = 0`$ and $`\lvert q\rvert \lesssim 10^{-162}`$ it is NaN.

<sup>‡</sup> Ill-conditioned near the zeros.

<sup>§</sup> $`A = \sqrt{y^2 + y'^2 / w}`$ for $`y = \mathrm{ce}_m`$ or $`\mathrm{se}_m`$, and $`\sqrt{w y^2 + y'^2}`$ for $`y'`$, with $`w = \max(\lvert a - 2 q \cos 2x\rvert, 1)`$ and $`a`$ the characteristic value.

<sup>‖</sup> For $`2 q \cosh 2x > a`$, with $`a`$ the characteristic value: $`A = \sqrt{M^{(1)}(x)^2 + M^{(2)}(x)^2}`$ for the first and second kind $`M^{(j)}`$ (or their derivatives). For $`q \le 10^{-7}`$, the results are infinite for $`m \ge 4`$ (`modcem`) or $`m \ge 5`$ (`modsem`).

<sup>¶</sup> Coefficient $`k`$ of the output. For $`q \le 10^{-7}`$, only the largest coefficient and the two next to it are nonzero.

<sup>‡</sup> For $`q = \lambda \mp c^2 x^2 - m^2 / (1 - x^2) > 0`$ (prolate: $`-`$, oblate: $`+`$), with the characteristic value $`\lambda`$: $`A = \sqrt{S^2 + (1 - x^2) S'^2 / \hat q}`$ for the angular function $`S`$, and $`\sqrt{\hat q / (1 - x^2)}\,A`$ for $`S'`$, with $`\hat q = \max(q, ((1 - x^2) q'^2)^{1/3})`$.

<sup>§</sup> With the characteristic value `cv` rounded to the nearest f64.

<sup>‖</sup> For $`c^2 x^2 > \lambda \pm m^2 / (x^2 \mp 1)`$ (prolate: upper signs, oblate: lower): $`A = \sqrt{R_1^2 + R_2^2}`$ for the radial functions of the first and second kind $`R_1`$ and $`R_2`$, and $`\sqrt{R_1'^2 + R_2'^2}`$ for their derivatives.

<sup>*</sup> In the rows with $`\infty`$, each element is $`\pm\infty`$ with probability 1/4 (but at least one is finite), or only $`-\infty`$ for `softmax` and `log_softmax`, which can be NaN for $`+\infty`$, like in SciPy.

<sup>†</sup> Element $`j`$ or $`k`$ of the output.

<sup>‡</sup> Ill-conditioned near the zeros.

<sup>§</sup> Absolute error $`\sim 10^{-16}`$ at best, so the relative error is large for tiny probabilities.

<sup>‖</sup> The second column is SciPy.

<sup>¶</sup> The second column is SciPy, which wraps xsf here.

## Not covered

- Exact functions: `comb`, `comb_rep`, `factorial`, `multifactorial` (and `_checked`) on unsigned
  integers, where an error is a wrong integer, not a rounding error, and `round`, which is exact
- Complex input: `zeta` (NaN for non-real `z`, unless `q = 1`, where it is `riemann_zeta`)
- Other: `lanczos_sum_expg_scaled` (internal) and the `extended_*_error` metrics

## Method

- Each domain has $`N`$ seeded pseudo-random f64 inputs, uniform unless marked "(log)".
- The reference $`f`$ is computed with mpmath 1.4.1 at 50 or more digits, for the exact f64
  input, or with an alternative formula where mpmath is inaccurate (see
  `tools/accuracy/specs.py`).
- The error of $`y`$ is $`\lvert y - f\rvert / \lvert f\rvert`$ (of the complex values, for complex functions). Where
  a function oscillates, it is the smaller of that and $`\lvert y - f\rvert / A`$, with the local
  amplitude $`A`$ given below the table. A result is exact if both overflow to the same infinity,
  or both underflow ($`\lvert f\rvert`$ < 2.2e-308).
- **same** is rounded down, and counts any two NaNs as equal.
- Built on x86_64 Linux: xsf-rust with `rustc 1.100.0 (0ef1bc386 2026-11-09)` (release profile), xsf with `g++ (Ubuntu 13.3.0-6ubuntu2~24.04.1) 13.3.0`
  (`-O2 -std=c++17`).
- To regenerate, run `just accuracy` (needs `cargo`, `g++`, `uv`, and internet access to
  download xsf). The references are cached in `tools/accuracy/.cache/`.
